[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"602ca2","wall_time_seconds":0.6884021,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3941,"output":"import { useCallback, useLayoutEffect, useRef, useState } from \\"react\\";\\nimport { useReducedMotion } from \\"framer-motion\\";\\nimport { directionOf, splitWords, type Reading, type RelationKind, type Signal } from \\"./observatory\\";\\nimport \\"./observatory.css\\";\\n\\n/**\\n * The Signal Observatory: Spot the Relationship\'s world.\\n *\\n * The statement\'s words are stars on a dark field. On verdict the observatory\\n * draws the relationship as a figure between the actual indicator words, so the\\n * figure always points at text the student can read rather than at a position\\n * chosen for it.\\n *\\n * The three geometries are the drill\'s visual alphabet, and they are different\\n * shapes rather than different colours, because the discrimination this drill\\n * trains is causal against conditional and that has to survive a monochrome\\n * system.\\n */\\n\\nexport type SkyPhase = \\"arrival\\" | \\"drawn\\";\\n\\n/** One item\'s outcome, for the three seals. Real completions only. */\\nexport interface SealState {\\n  conditional: boolean;\\n  causal: boolean;\\n  comparative: boolean;\\n}\\n\\ninterface ObservatoryStageProps {\\n  signal: Signal;\\n  phase: SkyPhase;\\n  /** The reading of the student\'s answer. Absent before they commit. */\\n  reading?: Reading | null;\\n  /** Which types the student has completed. Omit and no seal lights. */\\n  seals?: SealState;\\n}\\n\\nconst CAPTION: Record<RelationKind, string> = {\\n  conditional: \\"If this, then this. A rule.\\",\\n  causal: \\"This makes that happen.\\",\\n  comparative: \\"More of this than that.\\",\\n};\\n\\nconst SEAL_ORDER: RelationKind[] = [\\"conditional\\", \\"causal\\", \\"comparative\\"];\\nconst SEAL_LABEL: Record<RelationKind, string> = {\\n  conditional: \\"Conditional\\",\\n  causal: \\"Causal\\",\\n  comparative: \\"Comparison\\",\\n};\\n\\ninterface Box {\\n  left: number;\\n  top: number;\\n  width: number;\\n  height: number;\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * One line of the statement as it actually set: where its letters start, and\\n * how much clear space sits between it and the line above. Every mark drawn\\n * for a word on this line has to fit inside that space, or it is drawn through\\n * the words of the line above.\\n */\\ninterface Line {\\n  top: number;\\n  room: number;\\n}\\n\\ninterface Layout {\\n  /** The glyph box of each word, field-relative. */\\n  boxes: Map<number, Box>;\\n  /** Which line each word set on. */\\n  lineOf: Map<number, number>;\\n  lines: Line[];\\n}\\n\\n/** Clear space assumed above the first line, which has the header above it. */\\nconst FIRST_LINE_ROOM = 22;\\n\\n/**\\n * The box of the word\'s letters rather than of its span. The span\'s box\\n * includes the line\'s half-leading and the star-point padding, so a figure\\n * placed from it started inside the line above.\\n */\\nfunction glyphRect(el: HTMLElement): DOMRect {\\n  const text = el.firstChild;\\n  if (text && text.nodeType === Node.TEXT_NODE && typeof document.createRange === \\"function\\") {\\n    const range = document.createRange();\\n    range.selectNodeContents(text);\\n    if (typeof range.getBoundingClientRect === \\"function\\") {\\n      const r = range.getBoundingClientRect();\\n      if (r.width > 0 && r.height > 0) return r;\\n    }\\n  }\\n  return el.getBoundingClientRect();\\n}\\n\\nexport function layoutOf(boxes: Map<number, Box>): Layout {\\n  const lineOf = new Map<number, number>();\\n  const lines: { top: number; bottom: number }[] = [];\\n  const ordered = [...boxes.entries()].sort((a, b) => a[0] - b[0]);\\n  for (const [i, b] of ordered) {\\n    const last = lines[lines.length - 1];\\n    // Words on one line share a top to within a pixel or two; a new line\\n    // starts well below the last one\'s top.\\n    if (!last || b.top > last.top + b.height / 2) {\\n      lines.push({ top: b.top, bottom: b.top + b.height });\\n    } else {\\n      last.top = Math.min(last.top, b.top);\\n      last.bottom = Math.max(last.bottom, b.top + b.height);\\n    }\\n    lineOf.set(i, lines.length - 1);\\n  }\\n  return {\\n    boxes,\\n    lineOf,\\n    lines: lines.map((l, n) => ({\\n      top: l.top,\\n      room: n === 0 ? FIRST_LINE_ROOM : Math.max(0, l.top - lines[n - 1].bottom),\\n    })),\\n  };\\n}\\n\\nconst EMPTY_LAYOUT: Layout = { boxes: new Map(), lineOf: new Map(), lines: [] };\\n\\nexport function ObservatoryStage({ signal, phase, reading, seals }: ObservatoryStageProps) {\\n  const still = useReducedMotion() ?? false;\\n  const drawn = phase === \\"drawn\\";\\n\\n  const fieldRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);\\n  const wordRefs = useRef(new Map<number, HTMLElement>());\\n  const [layout, setLayout] = useState<Layout>(EMPTY_LAYOUT);\\n  const [size, setSize] = useState({ width: 0, height: 0 });\\n\\n  // Words are laid out by text flow, so the only way to draw between them is to\\n  // measure where they landed. Re-measured on resize, because a reflow moves\\n  // every anchor and a figure left at the old coordinates would point at the\\n  // wrong word.\\n  const measure = useCallback(() => {\\n    const field = fieldRef.current;\\n    if (!field) return;\\n    const base = field.getBoundingClientRect();\\n    const next = new Map<number, Box>();\\n    for (const [i, el] of wordRefs.current) {\\n      const r = glyphRect(el);\\n      next.set(i, {\\n        left: r.left - base.left,\\n        top: r.top - base.top,\\n        width: r.width,\\n        height: r.height,\\n      });\\n    }\\n    setLayout(layoutOf(next));\\n    setSize({ width: base.width, height: base.height });\\n  }, []);\\n\\n  useLayoutEffect(() => {\\n    measure();\\n    if (typeof ResizeObserver === \\"undefined\\") return;\\n    const ro = new ResizeObserver(measure);\\n    if (fieldRef.current) ro.observe(fieldRef.current);\\n    return () => ro.disconnect();\\n  }, [measure, signal.order]);\\n\\n  // Words, with the character offset of each so anchors can be matched to them.\\n  const words = splitWords(signal.statement);\\n\\n  // Which word indices each relation anchors to.\\n  const anchorIndex = new Map<number, RelationKind>();\\n  for (const rel of signal.relations) {\\n    for (const a of rel.anchors) {\\n      for (const w of words) {\\n        if (w.start < a.end && a.start < w.end) anchorIndex.set(w.i, rel.kind);\\n      }\\n    }\\n  }\\n\\n  const drawnKinds = new Set(drawn ? (reading?.drawn ?? signal.relations.map((r) => r.kind)) : []);\\n  const ghostKinds = drawn ? (reading?.invented ?? []) : [];\\n\\n  return (\\n    <div\\n      className=\\"observatory flex h-full min-h-[560px] w-full flex-col gap-6 px-5 py-8 md:px-12\\"\\n      data-phase={phase}\\n      data-testid=\\"observatory\\"\\n    >\\n      {/* The dome itself: nebulae, fixed stars, gilt ribs, meridian, horizon.\\n          Pure scenery, aria-hidden, outside every tested container. */}\\n      <div className=\\"ob-room\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\">\\n        <div className=\\"ob-nebula\\" />\\n        <div className=\\"ob-stars\\" />\\n        <div className=\\"ob-dome\\" />\\n        <div className=\\"ob-meridian\\" />\\n        <div className=\\"ob-horizonline\\" />\\n      </div>\\n\\n      <div className=\\"flex items-center justify-between\\">\\n        <span className=\\"observatory-display text-sm text-[hsl(var(--ob-ink))]\\">\\n          {drawn ? \\"The figure\\" : \\"The field\\"}\\n        </span>\\n        <span className=\\"observatory-mono text-xs text-[hsl(var(--ob-ink-dim))]\\">\\n          Rung {signal.rung}\\n        </span>\\n      </div>\\n\\n      <div\\n        ref={fieldRef}\\n        className=\\"ob-field relative\\"\\n        data-arriving={!drawn ? \\"true\\" : \\"false\\"}\\n        data-testid=\\"ob-field\\"\\n      >\\n        {/* The leading is restated at md: Tailwind\'s text-2xl carries its own\\n            32px line height, which silently replaced leading-loose and left 6px\\n            between lines, too little for any figure to sit in. */}\\n        <p className=\\"observatory-type text-xl leading-[2.4] md:text-2xl md:leading-[2.4]\\">\\n          {words.map((w) => (\\n            <span\\n              key={w.i}\\n              ref={(el) => {\\n                if (el) wordRefs.current.set(w.i, el);\\n                else wordRefs.current.delete(w.i);\\n              }}\\n              className=\\"ob-word\\"\\n              data-anchor={anchorIndex.has(w.i) ? \\"true\\" : undefined}\\n              data-drawn={\\n                anchorIndex.has(w.i) && drawnKinds.has(anchorIndex.get(w.i)!) ? \\"true\\" : undefined\\n              }\\n              data-testid={anchorIndex.has(w.i) ? \\"ob-anchor-word\\" : \\"ob-word\\"}\\n            >\\n              {w.text}{\\" \\"}\\n            </span>\\n          ))}\\n        </p>\\n\\n        {/* The figures, over the field. Only drawn once there is something true\\n            to draw, and only between anchors that were actually measured. */}\\n        {drawn && size.width > 0 ? (\\n          <svg\\n            className=\\"pointer-events-none absolute inset-0 h-full w-full overflow-visible\\"\\n            aria-hidden=\\"true\\"\\n            data-testid=\\"ob-figures\\"\\n          >\\n            {signal.relations\\n              .filter((r) => drawnKinds.has(r.kind))\\n              .map((r, n) => (\\n                <Figure\\n                  key={`${r.kind}-${n}`}\\n                  kind={r.kind}\\n                  indicators={r.indicators}\\n                  segments={segmentsFor(r.anchors, words, layout)}\\n                  still={still}\\n                />\\n              ))}\\n            {ghostKinds.map((kind) => (\\n              <Ghost key={kind} kind={kind} width={size.width} line={layout.lines[0]} />\\n            ))}\\n          </svg>\\n        ) : null}\\n      </div>\\n\\n      {drawn ? (\\n        <div className=\\"flex flex-col gap-1.5\\" data-testid=\\"ob-captions\\">\\n          {signal.relations\\n            .filter((r) => drawnKinds.has(r.kind))\\n            .map((r, n) => (\\n              <p key={n} className=\\"ob-caption observatory-mono text-xs\\" data-kind={r.kind}>\\n                {CAPTION[r.kind]} · {r.indicators.map((i) => `\\"${i}\\"`).join(\\" \\")}\\n              </p>\\n            ))}\\n          {reading?.missed.length ? (\\n            <p className=\\"ob-caption text-sm\\" data-testid=\\"ob-missed\\">\\n              Still undrawn: {reading.missed.join(\\", \\")}. The figure is there in the sentence.\\n            </p>\\n          ) : null}\\n        </div>\\n      ) : null}\\n\\n      <Seals seals={seals} />\\n    </div>\\n  );\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * One stretch of a figure: the anchors of one relation that set on the same\\n * line. A relation whose indicators wrap onto two lines gets one stretch per\\n * line rather than a chord cut diagonally through the text between them.\\n */\\ninterface Segment {\\n  x1: number;\\n  x2: number;\\n  /** Where the letters of this line start. Marks sit above this. */\\n  top: number;\\n  /** Clear space above the line. Marks never rise further than this. */\\n  room: number;\\n  /** How many anchor words this stretch spans. */\\n  count: number;\\n}\\n\\nexport function segmentsFor(\\n  anchors: { start: number; end: number }[],\\n  words: ReturnType<typeof splitWords>,\\n  layout: Layout,\\n): Segment[] {\\n  const byLine = new Map<number, Segment>();\\n  const seen = new Set<number>();\\n  for (const a of anchors) {\\n    for (const w of words) {\\n      if (!(w.start < a.end && a.start < w.end) || seen.has(w.i)) continue;\\n      seen.add(w.i);\\n      const box = layout.boxes.get(w.i);\\n      const n = layout.lineOf.get(w.i);\\n      if (!box || n === undefined) continue;\\n      const line = layout.lines[n];\\n      const seg = byLine.get(n);\\n      if (!seg) {\\n        byLine.set(n, { x1: box.left, x2: box.left + box.width, top: line.top, room: line.room, count: 1 });\\n      } else {\\n        seg.x1 = Math.min(seg.x1, box.left);\\n        seg.x2 = Math.max(seg.x2, box.left + box.width);\\n        seg.count += 1;\\n      }\\n    }\\n  }\\n  return [...byLine.entries()].sort((a, b) => a[0] - b[0]).map(([, seg]) => seg);\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * One relationship\'s geometry.\\n *\\n * Conditional is a single hard shaft, causal is doubled and heavier, and\\n * comparative is a beam that tips. The arrowhead on a conditional appears only\\n * when the indicator\'s direction is known: an arrow pointing the wrong way\\n * teaches the reversal error.\\n */\\nfunction Figure({\\n  kind,\\n  indicators,\\n  segments,\\n  still,\\n}: {\\n  kind: RelationKind;\\n  indicators: string[];\\n  segments: Segment[];\\n  still: boolean;\\n}) {\\n  if (!segments.length) return null;\\n\\n  // Each stretch arcs over its own words, inside the clear space above its\\n  // line: the base sits just over the letters and the peak never rises past\\n  // the gap, so nothing is ever drawn through the line above.\\n  const shapes = segments.map((seg) => {\\n    const base = seg.top - 3;\\n    const lift = Math.max(3, Math.min(10, seg.room - 9));\\n    const d = `M ${seg.x1} ${base} Q ${(seg.x1 + seg.x2) / 2} ${base - 2 * lift} ${seg.x2} ${base}`;\\n    return { seg, base, d, len: Math.ceil(seg.x2 - seg.x1 + 4 * lift + 24) };\\n  });\\n\\n  const direction = kind === \\"conditional\\" ? directionOf(indicators[0]) : null;\\n  const first = shapes[0];\\n  const last = shapes[shapes.length - 1];\\n  const head =\\n    direction === \\"necessary\\" ? { x: first.seg.x1, y: first.base } : { x: last.seg.x2, y: last.base };\\n  const showHead = kind === \\"causal\\" || (kind === \\"conditional\\" && direction !== null);\\n\\n  return (\\n    <g data-testid={`ob-figure-${kind}`} data-kind={kind}>\\n      {shapes.map(({ d, len }, n) => (\\n        <path\\n          key={n}\\n          className=\\"ob-figure\\"\\n          data-kind={kind}\\n          data-draw={still ? undefined : \\"true\\"}\\n          d={d}\\n          style={\\n            still\\n              ? undefined\\n              : ({ strokeDasharray: len, [\\"--ob-len\\" as string]: len } as React.CSSProperties)\\n          }\\n        />\\n      ))}\\n      {/* Causal is doubled: a second shaft just above the first, so the push is\\n          visible in the line\'s weight rather than in a colour. Above, not\\n          below: below is where the letters are. */}\\n      {kind === \\"causal\\"\\n        ? shapes.map(({ d }, n) => (\\n            <path\\n              key={`echo-${n}`}\\n              className=\\"ob-figure\\"\\n              data-kind=\\"causal\\"\\n              d={d}\\n              transform=\\"translate(0,-3)\\"\\n              opacity={0.6}\\n            />\\n          ))\\n        : null}\\n      {/* Comparative gets a level beam under each stretch that spans two terms.\\n          Level, because the figure does not know which term is greater, and an\\n          \\"as strong as\\" has no greater term at all. */}\\n      {kind === \\"comparative\\"\\n        ? shapes\\n            .filter(({ seg }) => seg.count > 1)\\n            .map(({ seg, base }, n) => (\\n              <g key={`beam-${n}`} className=\\"ob-beam\\" data-testid=\\"ob-beam\\">\\n                <line className=\\"ob-figure\\" data-kind=\\"comparative\\" x1={seg.x1} y1={base} x2={seg.x2} y2={base} />\\n              </g>\\n            ))\\n        : null}\\n      {showHead ? (\\n        <polygon\\n          className=\\"ob-arrowhead\\"\\n          data-testid={`ob-head-${kind}`}\\n          points={`${head.x},${head.y} ${head.x - 4},${head.y - 7} ${head.x + 4},${head.y - 7}`}\\n        />\\n      ) : null}\\n    </g>\\n  );\\n}\\n\\n/** A relationship the student named that is not in the statement. */\\nfunction Ghost({ kind, width, line }: { kind: RelationKind; width: number; line?: Line }) {\\n  // Over the first line, inside the space above it, so the dissolving shape\\n  // never sits on the words the student is reading.\\n  const base = (line?.top ?? FIRST_LINE_ROOM) - 4;\\n  return (\\n    <path\\n      className=\\"ob-figure\\"\\n      data-kind={kind}\\n      data-ghost=\\"true\\"\\n      data-testid={`ob-ghost-${kind}`}\\n      d={`M ${width * 0.2} ${base} Q ${width * 0.5} ${base - 26} ${width * 0.8} ${base}`}\\n    />\\n  );\\n}\\n\\n/** The three instrument dials. Lit by real completions only. */\\nfunction Seals({ seals }: { seals?: SealState }) {\\n  return (\\n    <div className=\\"mt-auto flex flex-wrap gap-2 pt-4\\" data-testid=\\"ob-seals\\">\\n      {SEAL_ORDER.map((k) => (\\n        <span\\n          key={k}\\n          className=\\"ob-seal observatory-mono rounded-sm px-3 py-1.5 text-xs\\"\\n          data-kind={k}\\n          data-lit={seals?.[k] ? \\"true\\" : undefined}\\n          aria-label={`${SEAL_LABEL[k]}: ${seals?.[k] ? \\"answered correctly\\" : \\"not yet answered correctly\\"}`}\\n          data-testid={`ob-seal-${k}`}\\n        >\\n          {SEAL_LABEL[k]}\\n        </span>\\n      ))}\\n    </div>\\n  );\\n}\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 19.7 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Warning: truncated output (original token count: 14325)\nTotal output lines: 3\n\n{"chunk_id":"c6a212","wall_time_seconds":0.5636437,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":9519,"output":"{\\r\\n  \\"task\\": \\"Tell me what kind of relationship this sentence contains and the exact word or words that create it. If there is more than one, name them all.\\",\\r\\n  \\"coach\\": {\\r\\n    \\"drill\\": \\"a relationship-identification drill: the student names whether a statement is conditional, causal or comparative, and quotes the exact word or words that create it\\",\\r\\n    \\"rubric\\": [\\r\\n      \\"A correct answer has two parts and needs both: the relationship type, and the exact indicator word or words from the statement. Type alone is not a complete answer.\\",\\r\\n      \\"A statement can carry more than one relationship. When it does, every relationship must be named with its own indicators, and naming a relationship that is not there is also wrong.\\",\\r\\n      \\"The strongest causal indicator is often a verb rather than a connective. Raises, drives, yields, starves, dries, lowers and homogenizes are causal words, and a student who quotes one has found the right thing.\\",\\r\\n      \\"Requirement language is conditional, not causal. Requires, depends on, contingent on and provided name what must be true; they do not claim that anything made anything happen.\\",\\r\\n      \\"Accept any reasonable quotation of the indicator. Do not penalise capitalisation, a slightly wider or narrower span, or a student who writes only if as only-if.\\",\\r\\n      \\"Comparisons can use a stated second term, a numeric benchmark, or a comparison standard recoverable from context. Do not require two named objects or the word than. Heavier contrasts with the light bell in item 31; longer contrasts with the hard-mortar case in item 36; higher still contrasts with the previous pressure in item 47. Plain adjectives such as deep and adverbs such as efficiently alone are not comparisons.\\",\\r\\n      \\"The explanation count groups relationship types, not every separate claim. Multiple comparisons or causal claims can share one type. Accept any valid indicator for a credited type, including a narrower quoted span, but check that the student pairs it with the right type.\\"\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    \\"partial_rule\\": \\"Mark \'partial\' when the student names the right type with wrong or missing indicators, quotes the credited indicators but labels the type wrongly, or finds some but not all of the relationships in a multi-relationship statement. Mark \'incorrect\' only when both parts miss.\\",\\r\\n    \\"grading_rubric\\": [\\r\\n      \\"Grade each relationship as a pair: the type and its indicator words together. Only a reveal earns that. */\\n  revealed: boolean;\\n  /**\\n   * When true, the item is finished one way or another. The authored\\n   * explanation shows from here on, including on a correct answer: it names the\\n   * indicator word and the discrimination the item teaches, and withholding it\\n   * from the students who are getting it right is backwards.\\n   */\\n  resolved: boolean;\\n  /**\\n   * What to call the credited answers after the first. Authored per Activity,\\n   * because the generic wording is wrong wherever the second answer is not an\\n   * alternative phrasing: in Conditional Rephrase it is the contrapositive, the\\n   * other half of what the task asked for.\\n   */\\n  secondaryLabel?: string;\\n  item: ActivityItem;\\n}\\n\\nconst DEFAULT_SECONDARY_LABEL = \\"Also accepted\\";\\n\\nconst VERDICT_STYLE: Record<FeedbackState, { label: string; chip: string; dot: string }> = {\\n  correct: {\\n    label: \\"Correct\\",\\n    chip: \\"bg-[hsl(var(--state-correct)/0.12)] text-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]\\",\\n    dot: \\"bg-[hsl(var(--sta…1645 tokens truncated…hole stimulus, cut into chunks.\\n *\\n * `prompt` is the entire stimulus and is never shown whole to the student; she\\n * only ever sees one chunk at a time, and only until she taps past it. It is\\n * stored whole because the chunks are checked against it and because the final\\n * cumulative round is scored against everything in it.\\n *\\n * `credited_answers` holds one entry: a model retelling of the whole stimulus\\n * in plain words with every detail intact. It is the reference for that final\\n * cumulative round, and the clearest single statement of what \\"close enough\\"\\n * means here. Per-chunk there is no credited answer, only `details`.\\n */\\nexport interface RecallItem extends ActivityItem {\\n  chunks: RecallChunk[];\\n  /**\\n   * The chunk numbers after which the student says back everything so far with\\n   * nothing on screen. The load grows every round, which is the point of the\\n   * drill and the part software can enforce that paper cannot.\\n   */\\n  cumulative_after: number[];\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * How a spoken-recall Activity is run.\\n *\\n * `stimulus` is v1: one stimulus at a time, with cumulative rounds. `section`\\n * is the captured drill, where the unit of work is a whole LR section run\\n * unbroken against a clock, with no cumulative round and no coach in the loop.\\n * Absent means `stimulus`, so every Activity authored before section mode keeps\\n * behaving exactly as it did.\\n */\\n/**\\n * `section-library` is the door: an Activity whose content is the list of\\n * sections a student picks from, rather than a section itself. It carries no\\n * `source_section` and no clock, which is why the database exempts it from the\\n * completeness check that a real section must satisfy.\\n */\\nexport type ActivityMode = \\"stimulus\\" | \\"section\\" | \\"section-library\\";\\n\\n/**\\n * The Read\'s type, as captured: Loophole, Implication, Contradiction, Rephrase.\\n * Stored as the single captured letter rather than a spelled-out name, because\\n * the key is ground truth and re-spelling it invites drift.\\n */\\nexport type TheReadType = \\"L\\" | \\"I\\" | \\"C\\" | \\"R\\";\\n\\n/**\\n * The minutes a section is meant to take, per accommodation. Time is a scored\\n * criterion, so the goal cannot be one number: a student with 53% or 70% extra\\n * time is not slow when she takes longer, and grading her against the standard\\n * goal would score her accommodation as a failure.\\n */\\nexport interface TimeGoalMinutes {\\n  standard: number;\\n  accommodation_53: number;\\n  accommodation_70: number;\\n}\\n\\n/** One captured LR question that hangs off a stimulus. */\\nexport interface SectionQuestion {\\n  /** e.g. \\"PT105.S2.Q1\\". */\\n  citation: string;\\n  qnum: number;\\n  question_type: string;\\n  correct_answer: string;\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * One stimulus of a section run.\\n *\\n * `credited_answers` and `cumulative_after` are both empty here, and that is\\n * the design rather than missing content: the captured drill has no cumulative\\n * round, so there is nothing for a reference retelling to be the reference for.\\n * The Read is scored against `the_read`, which is captured ground truth and\\n * strictly better than an authored retelling.\\n *\\n * Two questions can share one stimulus, which is why `questions` is a list and\\n * why a 25-question section is 23 items. She recalls the stimulus once.\\n */\\nexport interface RecallSectionItem extends ActivityItem {\\n  /** e.g. \\"PT105_S2_Q1\\"; the recording and transcript are named from it. */\\n  slug: string;\\n  chunks: RecallChunk[];\\n  cumulative_after: number[];\\n  /** The captured Read for this stimulus, said at the end of it. */\\n  the_read: string;\\n  the_read_type: TheReadType;\\n  /** Path to Joshua\'s recording of the process done out loud. */\\n  model_recording: string;\\n  model_transcript: string;\\n  questions: SectionQuestion[];\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * Per-Activity judging brief for the Coach. Each Activity trains a different\\n * skill and therefore needs a different rubric: what counts as `correct` in\\n * Conditional Rephrase is not what counts in Dumb it Down. Without this the\\n * edge function falls back to the Negation Station rubric it shipped with,\\n * which would misjudge every other Activity.\\n */\\nexport interface ActivityCoach {\\n  /** One line naming the drill and the skill, e.g. \\"a conditional-translation drill\\". */\\n  drill: string;\\n  /** The judging rules, most important first. Sent verbatim to the model. */\\n  rubric: string[];\\n  /** What separates `partial` from `incorrect` in THIS Activity. */\\n  partial_rule: string;\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * The long-form breakdown on an Activity\'s front door: what the drill actually\\n * trains, why it is built the way it is, and what doing it properly looks like.\\n *\\n * The numbered `rules` are the procedure and answer \\"what do I press\\". They do\\n * not answer \\"why am I doing this\\", and for a drill whose whole design is\\n * counter-intuitive -- the text is taken away on purpose, a summary is a\\n * failure, going slowly is correct -- a student who does not have the why will\\n * quietly do the easier thing instead.\\n */\\n/**\\n * One beat of a worked demonstration: a line of the stimulus, then what the\\n * student would say out loud about it.\\n */\\nexport interface BriefingBeat {\\n  /** \\"Read\\", \\"Tell it back\\", \\"Colour it\\", \\"The Recall\\". */\\n  kind: string;\\n  text: string;\\n}\\n\\nexport interface ActivityBriefing {\\n  /** The line under the section label, before the sections. */\\n  lede?: string;\\n  /**\\n   * A section is a heading plus prose, and optionally either a numbered\\n   * procedure or a worked demonstration.\\n   *\\n   * Both were added for the section drill, whose briefing is the first thing a\\n   * student ever reads about the method and therefore has to teach it rather\\n   * than remind her of it. Prose alone made the procedure something she had to\\n   * extract from a paragraph, and the one thing that makes an unfamiliar\\n   * process click is seeing somebody do it once. Both are optional, so every\\n   * Activity authored before this renders exactly as it did.\\n   */\\n  sections: {\\n    heading: string;\\n    body: string[];\\n    /**\\n     * A numbered procedure. Rendered as an ordered list, after the prose.\\n     *\\n     * `short` is the one-line definition of the step, for the surfaces that\\n     * cannot carry `body`: the walkthrough card mid-run, and anywhere else the\\n     * method has to be restated in a sentence. Authored here so a step can only\\n     * be redefined…352 tokens truncated… Absent on legacy Activities; the Coach then uses its negation default. */\\n  coach?: ActivityCoach;\\n}\\n\\n/** A row of `activities` (admin-owned definition library). */\\nexport interface Activity {\\n  id: string;\\n  slug: string;\\n  title: string;\\n  subtitle: string | null;\\n  instructions: ActivityInstructions;\\n  item_format: ItemFormat;\\n  items: ActivityItem[];\\n  /** Absent (null) on every Activity authored before section mode. */\\n  mode: ActivityMode | null;\\n  time_goal_minutes: TimeGoalMinutes | null;\\n  /** e.g. \\"PT105.S2\\". Null unless this Activity is one captured section. */\\n  source_section: string | null;\\n  created_by: string;\\n  class_id: string;\\n  created_at: string;\\n  updated_at: string;\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * A list row: everything a card draws, and nothing else.\\n *\\n * The list used to select `*`, which carries each assignment\'s whole `items`\\n * snapshot -- roughly 30KB for Conditional Rephrase alone -- so that a card\\n * could render a title, a status, a score and a count. The count is the only\\n * thing it ever needed from that payload, and the database can do the counting.\\n *\\n * The list and the runner deliberately stop sharing a shape here. They never\\n * needed the same data, and the shared type is what made the waste invisible.\\n */\\nexport interface ActivityAssignmentSummary {\\n  /** Absent until source revocation is installed; independent of progress. */\\n  revoked_at?: string | null;\\n  revoked_by?: string | null;\\n  /** Section mode fields, so a list can tell an Attempt from other work. */\\n  mode?: ActivityMode | null;\\n  source_section?: string | null;\\n  id: string;\\n  activity_id: string | null;\\n  student_id: string;\\n  title: string;\\n  subtitle: string | null;\\n  status: ActivityStatus;\\n  score: number | null;\\n  score_numerator?: number | null;\\n  score_judged?: number | null;\\n  score_total?: number | null;\\n  scoring_version?: number | null;\\n  /**\\n   * Kept, unlike `items`. The Classroom hero draws its authored tagline from\\n   * here, and the whole object is a few hundred bytes against the snapshot\'s\\n   * thirty thousand.\\n   */\\n  instructions: ActivityInstructions;\\n  created_at: string;\\n  started_at: string | null;\\n  completed_at: string | null;\\n  /** How many items the run holds. Counted in the database, not shipped. */\\n  item_count: number;\\n}\\n\\n/** A row of `activity_assignments` (per-student snapshot). */\\nexport interface ActivityAssignment {\\n  revoked_at?: string | null;\\n  revoked_by?: string | null;\\n  id: string;\\n  activity_id: string | null;\\n  student_id: string;\\n  title: string;\\n  subtitle: string | null;\\n  instructions: ActivityInstructions;\\n  item_format: ItemFormat;\\n  items: ActivityItem[];\\n  status: ActivityStatus;\\n  score: number | null;\\n  /**\\n   * The score\'s parts, kept so the screen can name the denominator it used.\\n   * `score` alone cannot distinguish \\"88% of 59 checked\\" from \\"88% of 62\\", and\\n   * the Activity card renders a bare number.\\n   */\\n  score_numerator?: number | null;\\n  score_judged?: number | null;\\n  score_total?: number | null;\\n  /**\\n   * 1 = scored over every item, so an unjudged item counted as wrong. 2 = scored\\n   * over the judged items only. The clock is what her\\n   * time is scored against, so reading it live from `activities` would let a\\n   * later edit rescore a run she has already finished.\\n   */\\n  mode?: ActivityMode | null;\\n  time_goal_minutes?: TimeGoalMinutes | null;\\n  source_section?: string | null;\\n  started_at: string | null;\\n  completed_at: string | null;\\n  assigned_by: string;\\n  class_id: string;\\n  created_at: string;\\n}\\n\\n/** A row of `activity_item_responses` (append-only attempt log). */\\nexport interface ActivityItemResponse {\\n  id: string;\\n  assignment_id: string;\\n  student_id: string;\\n  item_order: number;\\n  response_text: string;\\n  verdict: Verdict | null;\\n  attempt_no: number;\\n  created_at: string;\\n}\\n\\n/** Structured result returned by the activity-coach edge function. */\\nexport interface CoachVerdict {\\n  verdict: Verdict;\\n  feedback: string;\\n  /**\\n   * For drills that send a numbered checklist, which of those ideas the\\n   * response carried. Undefined means nobody judged coverage, which is not the\\n   * same as none surviving, and surfaces as an unticked checklist.\\n   */\\n  covered?: number[];\\n}\\n\\n/** Max Coach attempts before the credited answer reveals. */\\nexport const MAX_ATTEMPTS = 2;\\n\\n/**\\n * One stimulus on the Report Card.\\n *\\n * Mirrors what `grade-rrc-section` writes. `percent` is Coverage over that\\n * stimulus\'s own Details; `band` is what the student is actually shown, because\\n * a correct run is not 100% and a raw percentage against an implied maximum\\n * misreports it (ADR 0018).\\n */\\nexport interface SectionStimulusGrade {\\n  slug: string;\\n  /** Authored position in the section. ## assistant

The exported grading contract is **not fully consumed by the source snapshot**. At `origin/main = 3389858b`, no Spot the Relationship files changed after `916f195e`; these are residual contract gaps, not regressions in that interval.

1. **[P1] The authoritative structured key never reaches grading or receipt identity.**  
   [SQL migration:98](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str/supabase/migrations/20261001120000_activity_ai_admission_v1.sql:98) builds the canonical payload without `answer_key`. [grading.ts:47](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str/supabase/functions/activity-coach/grading.ts:47) consequently cannot pass relationships, `connects`, `why`, traps, benchmarks, or `required_parts` to the model. A key-only edit also leaves the payload hash unchanged, permitting reuse of an obsolete cached judgment and escaping dispatch’s source-change check.  
   **Fix:** validate and include the complete key in canonical payloads, prompt assembly, and first-attempt source comparisons.

2. **[P1] The eight new grading rules are ignored.**  
   [grading.ts:79](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str/supabase/functions/activity-coach/grading.ts:79) spreads only `coach.rubric`. The exported [grading rubric:1649](C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/str-current-live.json:1649) supplies explicit correct/partial/incorrect boundaries, contradictory-pair handling, split-comparison completeness, and first-failure hint restrictions. Although SQL carries the whole coach object, those rules never enter the prompt. The generic first-attempt instruction at line 93 does not explicitly prohibit listing missing types or quoting individual key indicators.  
   **Fix:** establish explicit rubric precedence and consume the new rules, retaining intentional legacy fallback.

3. **[P2] Item 36’s trap contradicts the acceptance rule.**  
   [Export:1058](C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/str-current-live.json:1058) calls identifying only `softer than` as comparative a trap. But [export:1651](C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/str-current-live.json:1651) requires at least one valid indicator per type, and [observatory.ts:320](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str/src/features/activity-centre/worlds/observatory/observatory.ts:320) uses that same rule. Thus `conditional, when; comparative, softer than; causal, drives` qualifies for instant correctness but could be penalized once traps reach the coach.  
   **Fix:** describe `longer` as another valid comparison to notice, without making its omission a grading fault.

4. **[P2] The active old rubric misstates `provided`.**  
   [Source content:13](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str/src/features/activity-centre/content/spot-the-relationship.json:13) groups `provided` with words naming what “must be true.” The exported keys correctly treat it as sufficient, particularly items 33 and 45. This creates conflicting guidance about conditional direction.  
   **Fix:** separate sufficient-condition language from necessary-condition language in the rubric.

5. **[P2] Final feedback ignores the richer explanations.**  
   [CoachFeedback.tsx:96](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str/src/features/activity-centre/components/CoachFeedback.tsx:96) renders only legacy `item.explanation`. Items such as 29 still finish with “The first sentence that carries all three,” despite the export containing explanations of each connection. [grading.ts:94](C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str/supabase/functions/activity-coach/grading.ts:94) merely permits greater explicitness on attempt two; it does not require a complete final explanation.  
   **Fix:** render authored structured explanations after resolution/reveal, covering each type, indicator, and connection.

The 48-key consistency check passed: **48 unique orders, 88 relationships, 109 indicator entries**, matching legacy types and indicators. Every quoted indicator word exists in its prompt. Prompts, credited answers, and legacy explanations match source.
